### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
something like 1.8-2%. It’s likely that predictions of a fall
in the unemployment rate will be pushed out such that
it ends 2026 in the 5.3-5.5% range – hence no fall in the
unemployment rate this year.
Greater economic slack than previously expected will
help mitigate the risks to inflation over the medium term.
Even so, the RBNZ’s forecasts for the OCR are likely to
be revised up. First, we suspect that the terminal OCR

will rise from the current 3% to something more like
3.2%, reflecting higher inflation expectations coming
from the more elevated inflation forecast profile. More
immediately, we suspect that the December 2026 quarter
projection will lift by around 40-50bp to around 2.8%.
This would signal two 25bp rises and a chance of a further
increase through the balance of 2026.
Technically, one may be able to divine the implied
probability of an increase at the next meeting in July

from the RBNZ’s published forecast for the September
quarter OCR. As a guide, a 25% chance of a 25bp hike
in July would be consistent with an average OCR in the
September quarter of 2.39%, whereas a 50% probability
would deliver 2.44%. We are cautious in interpreting the
forecast in this way, as it may be that we see a smoothed
model-determined path for the OCR at this near horizon,
rather than a path that has been set as an explicit signal
to the market. It would be best to read the Summary

Record of Meeting and the commentary in the press
conference to get the best guide on what the RBNZ
anticipates for the July meeting. Our view is that the
RBNZ could signal perhaps a 25% chance of a July move,
but that most MPC members will be content to withhold
judgment until September when more information will be
available (notably the June quarter CPI).
On that score, based on our reading of the April Record
of Meeting, we suspect that a vote will be required at

this meeting. Our sense is that there will still be two
members potentially interested in voting for a more
proactive approach.
Key communication messages will likely include
the following:
• The RBNZ will remain vigilant for signs on persistent
inflation and second-round inflation impacts and
will act decisively if such signs begin to emerge. This
message is inevitable given how high inflation is going
to be in the near term.
• Core inflation is currently inside the target range and

is expected to not drift too far from the 1-3% target
range. Medium-term expectations are likely viewed as
broadly anchored, and the RBNZ will emphasise that it
is important this remains the case.
• OCR increases are likely in 2026. Hence, they move
away from the February view that perhaps just one
increase might occur this year.
• The duration of the war is a key uncertainty that will
shape the inflation outlook and policy reaction function.
The RBNZ will likely emphasise that its policy stance can

be expected to evolve as the outlook becomes clearer,
and that the projections in the MPS are conditional on
assumptions that may be invalidated.
Scenarios.
The outcome of this meeting is likely to be a relatively
close-run thing, with the key point of contention likely
being around the pace at which future OCR increases
occur. We discussed the arguments that will likely be
raging around the table in our companion note released
earlier today. Given that baseline, we think the following

alternative scenarios are possible:
• A dovish scenario is one where the OCR remains
unchanged (possibly with no vote taken, as in April)
and the MPC does not change its language much from
the April Review. The terminal OCR might not rise far
at all, as implicitly the RBNZ would signal a belief that
inflation expectations remain anchored (in part due to
the pre-existing slack in the economy) and the Iran war
cost shock is transitory. The end-2026 OCR projection

might only rise modestly, as the implication would be
that a July hike is not really on the table as the issue
would be considered again in September.
• A hawkish scenario is one where the OCR is increased
and a sequence of follow-on 25bp increases is
signalled. This could be consistent with a 3% OCR in
September and perhaps slightly higher by the end
of the year. This scenario would suggest the RBNZ is
sufficiently concerned to get moving to neutral and

that the neutral rate itself might be noticeably higher
than 3%.
Kelly’s take.
I think the OCR should be increased at the May meeting.
The inflation outlook has shifted dramatically since the
second half of 2025 when the OCR was reduced below
3%. With the benefit of hindsight this should not have
occurred. A key element of the argument we made to
justify the 50bp cut last October was that it was an
insurance move that could be reversed if the outlook

changed. The outlook clearly has changed now that
headline inflation is set to move above 4% for the balance
of 2026. It would be much better to have the OCR near
neutral today. Hence beginning to get there now seems a
pressing priority.
I understand that the Iran war has delayed the economic
recovery. However, that isn’t in the mandate and there
are clear risks that this cost shock, coming so soon after a
long period of above-target inflation, will influence pricing

behaviour in the economy.

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
I am pessimistic that the Iran war will be quickly
resolved. Any positive news there should be considered
a contingency as opposed to part of the core forecast.
Headline inflation forecasts that assume a path for oil
prices anything like what the futures curve implies have
significant upside risks and should be discounted.
It’s unclear exactly how high the OCR will ultimately need
to go in 2027. But quickly getting the OCR towards neutral

would put the RBNZ in a good position to respond to
whatever happens in the future. Inflation and not growth
should be the priority.
Appendix: Key developments since the
February Monetary Policy Statement.
Activity: The December quarter GDP report was weaker
than the RBNZ’s expectations, with the economy growing
just 0.2% – 0.3ppts less than expected – and the level
of GDP 0.4% below expectations after accounting for
historical revisions. The RBNZ expected cumulative

growth of 1.6% over the first half of 2026, compared with
Westpac’s current forecast of growth of just 0.5%. The
implication is that the RBNZ’s estimate of the size of the
negative output gap is likely to rise in the near term.
Labour market: The unemployment rate edged down
to 5.3% in the March quarter, which was in line with the
RBNZ’s forecast. Employment growth of 0.2% was below
the RBNZ’s expectations, but this was offset by weaker-
than-expected labour force participation. Private sector

labour costs rose a subdued 0.4%q/q, which was in line
with the RBNZ’s forecast. We don’t think these data would
have shifted the dial much for the RBNZ.
Sentiment-based activity indicators: The NZIER’s
March quarter QSBO business survey reported an
unsurprising slump in business confidence, together
with smaller declines in firms’ expectations of activity,
hiring and investment. The ANZ’s monthly business
survey painted a similar picture in March and April (the

May survey will be released next week, just ahead of
the RBNZ’s meeting). The ANZ measure of consumer
confidence has also fallen sharply to a three-year low.
Housing market: The fall in consumer confidence
appears to have impacted the housing market in April,
with seasonally-adjusted sales declining about 4% (about
8% below year-earlier levels) and the average house
price declining 0.4% after a small increase during the
March quarter. Listing levels remain elevated at more

than decade-highs, especially in the main urban areas,
supported by reasonable levels of building activity at a
time of slow population growth. In February the RBNZ
forecast no growth in house prices this year. They are
unlikely to revise this forecast higher.
Inflation: Headline inflation remained at 3.1% in the year
to March, above the RBNZ’s February forecast of 2.8%
(and slightly above the revised forecast of 3.0% made at
the April OCR Review). Non-tradables inflation was just

0.1ppts above the RBNZ’s February forecast, with most of
the variation due to a 0.7ppt upside surprise in tradables
inflation (in part due to the sharp lift in fuel prices in
March). Measures of core inflation generally nudged lower
in the March quarter, but with most remaining in the
upper part of the RBNZ’s 1-3% target band.
Inflation expectations: The RBNZ’s survey of ‘expert’
forecasters pointed to a sharp rise in 1-year ahead
inflation expectations, a small rise in 2-year ahead

expectations (to 2.53%) and small falls in 5- and
10-year ahead expectations. The RBNZ’s survey of
consumers pointed to a notable lift in near-term inflation
expectations, but less so when it comes to medium-
term expectations. The RBNZ’s Business Survey pointed
to a sharp lift in 1-year ahead inflation expectations and
a smaller (26bps to 2.87%) increase in 2-year ahead
inflation expectations. However, 5-year expectations
nudged lower and 10-year ahead expectations fell 27bps.

Firms responding to the ANZ’s Business Survey indicate
that they expect wage growth to remain subdued.
Terms of trade: The SNA terms of trade were 0.7%
lower than expected in the December quarter. Given
developments in the Middle East – in particular, the
sharp rise in fuel prices – we estimate that the terms of
trade are likely to be more than 1% below the RBNZ’s
expectations in the current quarter despite the resilience
seen in New Zealand’s key export commodity prices.

Financial conditions: Domestic financial conditions have
mostly tightened since the RBNZ’s February meeting. For
example, the NZ50 equity index has declined 4.0%, the
2-year swap rate has increased 61bps to 3.57% and the
10-year bond rate has increased 37bps to 4.73%. Working
in the opposite direction, the trade-weighted exchange
rate index (TWI) has declined around 2%.
Fiscal policy: Budget 2026 will be delivered the day
after the RBNZ’s decision is delivered, but the Treasury’s

representative at the MPC meeting will have conveyed the
broad fiscal parameters that will be revealed on Budget
day. Based on our expectations for Budget 2026, we don’t
expect that the path of fiscal policy will have a significant
influence on the MPC’s deliberations at this meeting.

• Thanks to the AI supercycle, Taiwan is delivering remarkable economic and market
performance while gaining strategic importance in global supply chains
• Nominal GDP is set to enter the USD1tn club this year
• Real GDP growth is on track to approach 10%, making Taiwan Asia- 12’s fastest-
growing economy
• Per capita GDP is projected to reach US D45k, ranking Taiwan as Asia -12’s third-

richest economy
• The TAIEX has recently emerged as the world’s sixth-largest stock market
• For Singapore, Taiwan has overtaken China to become our largest trading partner
• Hidden challenges include K-shaped growth and widening wealth inequality
• Risks of stock market overheating, inflation, and monetary tightening are also drawing
close attention

The AI-driven export boom has sharply
boosted Taiwan’s growth performance.
Real GDP growth accelerated further to
13.7% YoY in 1Q from 12.7% in 4Q25,
marking the strongest quarterly expansion
since 1987.
P
er capital GDP is projected to reach
around USD45,000 in 2026, further
widening the income gap with Japan and
South Korea and reinforcing Taiwan’s
position as Asia-12’s third-richest
economy.

Ful
l-year 2026 GDP growth is on track to

Taiwan to outperform India and Vietnam
for a second consecutive year as Asia-
12’s fastest-growing economy.
No
minal GDP is on track to surpass the
USD1tn mark in 2026, bringing Taiwan
into the USD1tn club alongside China,
Japan, India, South Korea, and Indonesia.

### Page 3

Taiwan Chartbook: The AI trillion-dollar leap  May 12, 2026 
3

AI-driven export performance remains 
strong, although momentum may be 
starting to peak. Export growth moderated 
to 39.0% YoY in April, while real exports 
(excluding price effects) slowed more 
noticeably to 21.0%.

The evolving AI supply chain has shifted 
Taiwan’s import structure toward South 
Korea, particularly for HBM-related 
semiconductor supply. South Korea has 
become Taiwan’s second-largest import 
source since 2025, accounting for 13.2% 
of imports.

AI demand is increasingly redirecting 
Taiwan’s exports toward the US market. 
Since 2025, the US has overtaken China 
as Taiwan’s largest export destination, 
accounting for 31% of total exports.

The rise of AI is also elevating the 
strategic importance of Taiwan’s supply 
chain globally. On a single-country basis, 
Taiwan has overtaken China to become 
Singapore’s largest trading partner since 
2025, driven by a sharp increase in 
Singapore’s electronics imports from 
Taiwan.

### Page 4

Taiwan Chartbook: The AI trillion-dollar leap  May 12, 2026 
4

Investment indicators showed signs of 
peaking. Capital goods imports 
decelerated as capex by leading 
semiconductor firms moved past its peak, 
while building permits remained in 
negative territory amid continued 
weakness in the property market.

FDI sources remained broadly stable, with 
Europe continuing to be Taiwan’s largest 
source of inward investment, accounting 
for a 56% share in 2025.

FDI and ODI activity stayed buoyant. 
Approved FDI and ODI both surged by 
more than 100% YoY cumulatively in the 
first three months of this year, reaching 
USD6.1bn and USD32.8bn, respectively.

ODI destinations continued to shift away 
from China toward the America, with North 
America and Central & South America 
together accounting for 68% of Taiwan’s 
outbound investment flows in 2025.

### Page 5

Taiwan Chartbook: The AI trillion-dollar leap  May 12, 2026 
5

Consumption rebounded on the back of 
stimulus measures. Retail sales returned 
to positive growth from 4Q25 following the 
rollout of the cash handout programme.

Wage growth softened slightly and is likely 
to face further erosion from rising inflation 
ahead. Average regular earnings slowed 
to 2.6% YoY in March, slipping below the 
3% mark, while inflation-adjusted real 
wage growth eased to around 1%.

Labor market conditions remained broadly 
stable, although structural unemployment 
persisted. The unemployment rate held 
steady at 3.4% as of March, while youth 
unemployment among those aged 15-24 
remained elevated at 11.4%.

The AI-driven manufacturing boom has 
not generated significant employment 
gains due to the capital- and technology-
intensive nature of the sector. 
Employment growth continued to be 
driven mainly by the services sector rather 
than manufacturing.

CONSUMPTION / LABOR MARKET

### Page 6

Taiwan Chartbook: The AI trillion-dollar leap  May 12, 2026 
6

Export performance remained uneven 
between high-tech and traditional sectors. 
Exports of information and communication 
products and electronic components 
continued to post double-digit growth in 
April, while chemicals and metal exports 
remained in contraction.

The AI-driven tech cycle remained in 
expansionary territory, with the electronics 
inventory-to-shipment ratio staying healthy 
at around the neutral level of 1.0.

AI-driven manufacturing activity stayed 
robust. The electronics PMI rose further to 
67.5 in April, lifting the overall CIER 
manufacturing PMI to 60.3.

LNG supply disruptions stemming from the 
Middle East conflict have so far not 
caused significant power shortages in 
Taiwan. Electricity supply operating 
reserves remained at healthy levels of 
around 20% in early May.

Property market activity remained weak 
but showed signs of stabilization. The 
number of registered building transfers 
paused its 1.5-year contraction trend.

Transport indicators were mixed. Airport 
freight traffic remained steady and strong, 
while container throughput continued to 
contract.

Property prices also stabilized. Sinyi 
residential property prices in the Taipei 
area recorded a marginal 0.2% YoY 
increase in March.

The tourism sector continued to recover 
despite the absence of mainland Chinese 
visitors. Visitor arrivals reached 880k in 
March, approaching the pre-pandemic 
peak level of 1mn.

### Page 8

Taiwan Chartbook: The AI trillion-dollar leap  May 12, 2026 
8

The inflationary impact of the Middle East 
conflict has started to emerge. CPI rose to 
1.7% YoY in April, while the month-on-
month increase was more notable at 
0.5%.

Pipeline price pressures linked to the 
Middle East conflict remain strong.  
PMI price subindices, which typically lead 
CPI by around three months, surged 
sharply in April to levels close to the 2022 
peak seen during the Russia-Ukraine war.

Government energy subsidies through 
SOEs have helped cushion the shock, but 
they have not fully offset the inflation 
impact. Transportation costs led the 
increase in April CPI.

Inflation expectations are coming under 
close scrutiny. Consumer inflation 
expectations, which tend to move broadly 
in line with CPI, are likely to edge higher in 
the coming months.

Liquidity conditions remained favourable. 
M2 growth picked up to 5.8% YoY in 
March, staying in the upper half of the 
central bank’s reference range of 2.5-
6.5%. M1 growth accelerated more 
notably to 7.8%, resulting in a positive M1-
M2 gap.

Consumer lending remained restrained by 
the central bank’s credit control measures. 
Housing mortgage loans rose a moderate 
4.5% YoY in March, keeping overall 
consumer loan growth at 4.8%.

Corporate lending strengthened amid 
robust financing demand from tech 
companies. Loans to the electronics 
sector surged by more than 30% YoY in 
April, contributing to an 8.4% increase in 
total corporate loans.

Loan concentration risks improved 
marginally. Real estate-related loans 
accounted for 35.5% of banks’ total loan 
portfolios in March, down from the 2024 
peak of 37.5%.

LOAN MARKET

### Page 10

Taiwan Chartbook: The AI trillion-dollar leap  May 12, 2026 
10

Taiwan has overtaken Canada, the UK, 
and France this year to become the 
world’s sixth-largest stock market by 
market capitalization (USD4.7tn).

Foreign inflows into Taiwan’s equity 
market remain strong. Net foreign inward 
remittance into TWSE and TPEx rose by 
USD17.9bn in January-March, reaching a 
record high of USD338bn.

There are emerging signs of overheating. 
The TWSE market capitalization has 
exceeded 400% of nominal GDP, 
significantly deviating from historical 
trends – surpassing levels seen during the 
late-1980s bubble period.

Retail investor participation has also 
increased. New trading accounts on the 
TWSE rose to 102k in April, nearly 
doubling from 58k in December 2025.

## Document 243: UK asset markets starting to feel the heat

Source PDF: all report pdf/UK asset markets starting to feel the heat.pdf
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Article | 15 May 2026 1
THINK economic and financial analysis
Article  |  15 May 2026   FX   RATES   UNITED KINGDOM
UK asset markets starting to feel the
heat
UK markets were under pressure on Friday as the leadership battle
within the ruling Labour Party starts to open up. 30-year UK gilt yields
have risen to levels last seen in early 1998 and independent sterling
weakness is really starting to come through. A long hot summer for UK
politics warns of more to come

What’s happening
UK markets were under pressure on Friday as the leadership battle within the ruling Labour
Party starts to open up.
To recap, this week’s turmoil follows a very weak, but not totally unsurprising, set of local
election results for Labour earlier this month. With Nigel Farage’s Reform Party dominating the
polls, and the Green Party increasingly dividing Labour’s share of the vote, there’s growing
pressure on the party to change direction as it looks ahead to the next General Election in

three years’ time.
So far, formally at least, no leadership contest has been triggered. Keir Starmer remains both

### Page 2

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 15 May 2026 2
Labour leader and prime minister. But it is only a matter of time.
Wes Streeting, until this week the Health Secretary, has resigned and is expected to stand,
though it remains unclear whether he has support from the 81 Labour MPs required to trigger
a contest. Comments from Streeting today suggest he has acknowledged a leadership battle
will need to wait for the summer.
That’s because Andy Burnham, Mayor of Manchester and favourite to become Labour leader,

needs to be an MP in Westminster to be eligible to compete in a leadership contest. He has
announced he’ll be standing in Makerfield, a newly-vacated seat in the north of England, in a
by-election that’s likely to take place in the second half of June.
That in itself is no easy feat. Polls suggest that, in the absence of Burnham, Reform would win
comfortably in Makerfield. Farage’s party dominated the local elections in the area. Burnham
will be banking on his local popularity to get him elected.

Assuming he does, that opens up a leadership contest that's likely to take place through the
summer, with the results sewn up before the Labour Party conference in late September.
What gilt markets are watching
Until now, politics has not been the primary driver of gilt yields, which suggests there’s still
plenty of upside risk.
The Bank of England policy path has been lifted by higher oil prices over recent weeks. And we
also have the Bank’s Quantitative Tightening flooding the market with gilt supply at a pace of

£70bn per year. Higher US Treasury yields, amidst very healthy US market sentiment, is spilling
over to gilt yields, too.
But as of Friday, there are signs that political uncertainty is starting to fuel those Bank of
England rate hike expectations, too. Two rate hikes are priced by November and a third by
March 2027. And even more interestingly, markets are not pricing in subsequent rate cuts over
a two- or three-year horizon. Perceptions of the neutral rate – the level neither expansionary

nor restrictive for the economy – have been repriced higher.
That tells us investors are most focused on the impact a leadership contest might have on
Britain’s fiscal trajectory, gilt issuance over the next couple of years, and its interplay with
inflation. Burnham has said there’s a case for exempting defence spending from the main
fiscal rules, implying higher borrowing. Other Labour figures have flirted with extending the
timeline of when the fiscal rules need to be met – which again makes it easier to boost

borrowing in the near-term.
The biggest risk here is that investors begin to question the UK’s longer-term fiscal discipline.
Gilt markets rely on foreign investors and any signs that fiscal dynamics risk turning

### Page 3

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 15 May 2026 3
unsustainable could quickly turn sentiment. We’ve already seen that the uncertainty
around the Autumn Budget last November added a risk premium of around 25bp to 10Y gilts.
Right now, we estimate the premium to be around 10bp, higher than a few weeks ago, but still
less than around November’s budget.
Until we get a better understanding around the fiscal path forward, political risk premium is

likely to keep rising. A rise towards 5.30% is quite possible in the near term.
The reality might be different
With bond markets already under pressure, we suspect all leadership candidates will come
until intense pressure to rule out big changes to the fiscal rules over the next few weeks. The
memories of the 2022 mini-budget crisis haven’t gone away.
We’d also note that fiscal changes are unlikely before the Autumn Budget. And until then, the
Bank of England can only react to what is existing government policy. That suggests the

leadership drama is unlikely to drastically alter the BoE’s rate hiking path this summer.
Beyond politics, we also think Bank of England rate hike expectations look overblown. We
expect one rate hike this year, but more importantly perhaps, a return to rate cuts in 2027.
Once the near-term political noise has blown over, the direction for gilt yields is likely to be
downwards across the medium term. We’re forecasting the 10-year bond yield down to 4.5%

through the middle of next year.
EUR/GBP versus 10-year gilt-bund spread
Source: Refinitiv, ING
Sterling is dragged into the sell-off
What has been noticeable over the last week is that sterling has finally been dragged into this
political crisis. Up until recently, traded EUR/GBP volatility had been exceptionally low, near 4%,
and the FX option market's view over its direction had not really changed much at all over the

### Page 4

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 15 May 2026 4
last year. Now participants in the FX options market are prepared to pay 1.1% extra for the
right to buy over the right to sell EUR/GBP over the next three months. That is virtually
matching last year's 'Liberation Day' highs, when global equity markets were hit hard and the
growth-sensitive pound fell 5% against the euro.
Behind that recent stability in the pound might have been the BoE's relatively high policy rate

of 3.75% (helpful in a carry-seeking environment) and perhaps ongoing interest from M&A
activity. Year-to-date pending and completed target acquisitions of UK companies are up over
100% this year, compared to around 20% for Europe as a whole.
Yet the focus now shifts to the long, hot summer of the Labour leadership crisis and the
question is how far sterling could fall if international investors really turned bearish on the
pound. Over the years, we have found our Financial Fair Value model as a useful vehicle to

evaluate the size of risk premium that could emerge in EUR/GBP. The model looks at where
EUR/GBP is actually trading versus where it might be trading were typical relationships with
short-term rate differentials and equity performance to hold.
By our current reckoning, EUR/GBP is currently trading around 1% above fair value. As our
chart below shows, EUR/GBP can trade as much as 5% over fair value in extreme conditions –
such as in August 2019 when it looked like the UK would be leaving the EU without a deal. An

extra 4% on EUR/GBP today warns that 0.90 is not out of the question this summer for
EUR/GBP. For reference, the FX options market now attaches a 25% probability to EUR/GBP
trading to 0.90 within the next three months.
Our baseline is for a slightly more modest advance in EUR/GBP to 0.88/89 in late summer – but
the risks are clearly skewed to the upside.
EUR/GBP Financial Fair Value model
Source: ING calculations

## Document 244: United States_ Growth and Full Employment tested by Uncertainty (2026-04-27)

Source PDF: all report pdf/United States_ Growth and Full Employment tested by Uncertainty (2026-04-27).pdf
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 1st Quarter 2026 / April 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
GROWTH AND FULL EMPLOYMENT TESTED BY UNCERTAINTY
US growth remains robust, exhibiting strong momentum, but is still reliant on a narrow base – AI on the activity
side and healthcare for jobs. The energy shock presents a new challenge, and its impact will depend on both the
duration and severity of the Iran war. In any case, this situation is likely to drive inflation further above the target.

Our baseline scenario projects 2.4% annual GDP growth in 2026 (down 0.3pp vs. the pre-conflict outlook) and 2.5%
in 2027 (+0.3pp). Inflation is expected to reach 3.2% y/y in 2026. Against this backdrop, we expect the Fed to adopt a
two-sided stance, with balanced risks around the Fed Funds rate and a hold as the baseline scenario. Tariffs conti-
nue to pose significant uncertainty, as does the trajectory of the federal deficit, which is set to widen, notably due to

conflict-related outlays.
1 Summary of Economic Projections, Federal Reserve, March 2026.
GROWTH: WILL AI-LED OUTPERFORMANCE WITHSTAND HIGHER
ENERGY PRICES?
In 2026, growth should continue to be driven by the same factors as
in 2025 – AI-related CapEx, wealth effects stemming from high equity
valuations (until February), and healthcare outlays (up 0.4% to 6.0% of
GDP in 2025, with stability expected in 2026). AI diffusion, coupled with
near-stagnant employment, underpins productivity gains that signifi -

cantly exceed pre-pandemic trends (2.3% y/y on average since 2023,
vs. 1.4% from 2014 to 2019; see Chart 1). The Congressional Budget
Office (CBO) expects productivity gains to continue in 2026 (at 2.2%).
Nevertheless, growth remains narrowly based, with limited spillover
effects and a “K-shaped” investment pattern (see Chart of the Week
on this topic).
The Iran conflict introduces downside risks. Moreover, the US status as
a net energy exporter does not fully shield its economy from the effect

of higher inflation and rising costs on household purchasing power and
corporate profit margins (which are already under strain due to tariffs).
Consequently, GDP growth may be reduced by a few tenths in 2026,
depending on the duration and severity of the conflict. In summary, the
primary risk is not a shortfall relative to potential growth (1.8%), but
rather a more uneven economic landscape, characterised by increasing
fragility outside of AI-driven sectors.

A PUSH FOR BANK LENDING?
On 19 March, US banking regulators proposed a comprehensive re -
view of the regulatory framework. The primary objective is to encou -
rage banks to increase lending to households and businesses by redu -
cing the capital requirements associated with lending activities, and
to curb competition from the largely unregulated non-banking sector.
While the expected benefits are unlikely to materialise in the short
term (pending the finalisation and implementation of the regulations),

their impact in the medium to long term may be constrained by other
factors (paperwork and leverage requirements will remain unchanged,
and some of the released capital could be allocated to other activities).
More immediately, the uncertainties and inflationary pressures trig -
gered by the Middle East conflict are likely to dampen credit demand.
While 30-year mortgage rates had been trending downward since May
2025 (-90bp), they have rebounded by 25bp since late February, stan -

ding at 6.23% in late April. Therefore, the strong momentum observed
in H2 2025 for both outstanding household loans and corporate finan-
cing flows could run out of steam.
LOW PAYROLL GROWTH, YET FULL EMPLOYMENT PERSISTS
The labour market should remain close to full employment, with the
unemployment rate hovering at 4.3%-4.4%, compared with an esti -
mated long-term level of around 4.2% 1. Job creation has significantly
decelerated in recent quarters, deviating from the post-pandemic dy -

namism. In Q4 2025, non-farm payroll growth stood at 0.2% y/y, mar -
king the slowest rate (excluding recessions) since 2003. In addition, job
growth is increasingly concentrated in education and health services
(682k y/y in 2025, vs. 116k aggregate; see Chart 2). Those jobs are lar-
gely non-cyclical, which is positive, yet they are also low-value-added
(17% of employment, 9% of GDP). The low rate of layoffs and discharges
(1.1% in 2025, unchanged from 2024) reinforces the full employment

scenario. The US economy is effectively in a ‘low hiring, low firing’
regime, as firms are reluctant to both recruit and reduce staff amid
heightened uncertainty. Consequently, a slowdown linked to the Iran
conflict would likely only marginally increase the unemployment rate
– by 0.1pp to 0.3pp – compared with a flat baseline scenario.
INFLATION RETURNS TO CENTRE STAGE
CPI and core CPI are set to keep overshooting the 2% y/y target, at
least until 2028. The pass-through from higher oil prices, coupled with

partial, but likely, second-round effects from rising production costs, is
driving inflation forecasts upward. Firms have already absorbed tariff

Growth and inflation
SOURCE: BNP PARIBAS GLOBAL MARKETSCHART 1
2.8
2.1
2.4 2.3
3.0
2.7
3.3

EcoPerspectives / 1st Quarter 2026 / April 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
costs into their margins over the past year, leaving little scope for fur-
ther absorption. The uncertainty around this outlook remains high, hin-
ging on the trajectory, duration and severity of the Middle East conflict.
Any extension or escalation would further intensify price pressures.
That said, the inflationary spike is likely to be far less pronounced than

in 2022, when CPI peaked at 9.1% (June). Current conditions are, in
fact, less conducive to second-round effects, on both the supply and
demand sides (see Chart of the Week on this topic).
FOMC: TWO-SIDED OUTLOOK, STEADY POLICY RATE
Risks appear to be evenly distributed, given expectations of a slight
uptick in inflation alongside ongoing labour market concerns. In this
context, we project that the Fed Funds target range will hold steady at
3.5% - 3.75% until 2027. Nonetheless, we expect the FOMC to adopt a

two-sided outlook, signaling equal readiness to implement rate hikes
or cuts if necessary – marking a departure from the previous downward
bias. This stance would allow for a wait-and-see approach on core in-
flation and economic activity, while keeping market inflation expecta -
tions anchored and allowing for the possibility of a rapid policy shift.
As a result, policy will lean more on communication than on adjust -
ments in rates. This reflects the macroeconomic conditions that pre -

vailed prior to the shock. Compared to 2022, the policy rate is signifi -
cantly higher (3.75% upper bound in February 2026, vs. 0.25% in 2022),
while CPI inflation is considerably lower (2.9% y/y in Q4 2025, vs. 6.8%
in Q4 2021). Furthermore, the Fed tends to react more forcefully when
inflation is demand-driven rather than supply-driven (NBER Working
Paper, 2026), as monetary policy tools are more effective on the latter
(Powell, 2022).
Finally, the upcoming replacement of Jerome Powell (Chair since 2018)

does not alter our outlook. The incoming chair, Kevin Warsh, has re -
cently called for rate cuts, which is at odds with the “hawk” reputation
he earned during his governorship (2007–2011). However, the FOMC
will continue to be governed by majority rule, and we do not foresee a
dovish shift unless macroeconomic data dictate otherwise (see our Eco
Insight on this topic).
THE FISCAL RISK OF WAR
The US federal deficit is susceptible to a potential decline in the near

to mid-term, after narrowing to -5.9% of GDP (+0.4pp) in FY 2025, bols-
tered by student loan reform and additional tariff revenues. The latter
were expected to stabilise at around USD 300 bn annually, allowing
the budget deficit to hold at -5.8% of GDP in 2026 and 2027. Howe -
ver, the Supreme Court’s ruling that struck down “reciprocal” tariffs
could trigger refunds on duties collected in 2025. At the same time,
the Iran conflict is likely to exert pressure on public finances, primarily

through higher defence spending, while no tangible measures to sup -
port demand have been implemented so far.  The upward pressure on
bond yields is set to extend, particularly on the long-end of the curve
(10- and 30-year yields at 4.55% and 5.15% respectively by end-2026),
notably reflecting the fiscal risk of the conflict.
2 “Section 122 authorises the President to impose temporary import duties or surcharges «[w]henever fundamental international payments problems require special import

measures to restrict imports (1) to deal with large and serious United States balance-of-payments deficits” (Congress).
In summary, according to our forecasts, the federal deficit could widen
to -6.5% of GDP as early as this year. Should plans to ramp up military
spending materialise (President Donald Trump has called for a perma-
nent USD 500 bn annual increase, or 1.6% of GDP), against the back -
drop of ongoing war (and its associated costs), we anticipate that the

deficit could exceed -8.0% of GDP by 2027. Additionally, debt-service
costs, estimated at 3.3% of GDP in 2026–2027, could also rise accor -
dingly. In any case, federal debt remains on an upward trajectory and
is expected to exceed 100% of GDP as early as 2026.
FOREIGN TRADE: UNCERTAINTY PERSISTS
Following the Supreme Court’s ruling that terminated both “reci -
procal” and fentanyl-related tariffs, a universal 10% tariff (excluding
exemptions and bilateral agreements) has been implemented under

Section 122 of the Trade Act of 1974 2, in force until 24 July 2026. By
then, President Trump is expected to roll out a new tariff framework:
i) sector-specific measures under Section 232 of the Trade Expansion
Act of 1962 (pertaining to “national-security”); and ii) country-specific
measures, under Section 301 of the Trade Act of 1974 (addressing “un-
fair trade practices”). According to our estimates, the average effective
tariff rate is expected to reach at 8.4% to 10.6% in 2026, down from

10.1% in H2 2025, yet significantly higher than the 2.3% recorded at the
end of 2024. While uncertainty is set to persist (particularly regarding
the future of the USMCA), the US administration appears inclined to
uphold the trade agreements concluded in 2025, notably with the EU
and Japan.
Article completed on 23 April 2025
Anis Bensaidani
anis.bensaidani@bnpparibas.com
Céline Choulet
celine.choulet@bnpparibas.com

Strong productivity growth is driving economic growth

## Document 258: “来了”又“没完全来”？特斯拉官宣监督版FSD登陆中国，销售员：暂未收到落地通知！

Source PDF: all report pdf/“来了”又“没完全来”？特斯拉官宣监督版FSD登陆中国，销售员：暂未收到落地通知！.pdf
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

特斯拉官宣 FSD 监督版已在中国等 10 个国家和地区开放使⽤，但强调仍需驾驶员主动监
督，⻋辆并不能实现完全⾃动驾驶。⼀线销售⻔店尚未收到正式推送通知，⽬前仅部分试
驾⻋搭载 V13 版本供⽤⼾体验。有⻋主向官⽅客服咨询能否开通，得到的回应是： “ 正积极
推进，所有准备⼯作完成便会尽快推送。 ”
摘要：
继在中国市场发布多个⾯向 “ 智能驾驶 ” 的岗位后，5 ⽉ 21 ⽇，特斯拉官⽅对外公布了 FSD 监督版
（ Full Self-Driving Supervised ）的最新进展，称包括中国在内的 10 个国家和地区已开放使
⽤。
值得注意的是，特斯拉美国官⽹也同步显⽰了相关内容，同时强调监督版 FSD 仍需驾驶员主动
监督，⻋辆并不能实现完全⾃动驾驶。
图⽚来源：特斯拉美国官⽹
值得⼀提的是，⽇前，特斯拉中国发布多个与智能驾驶测试相关的招聘岗位，引发⾏业对 FSD
（ Full Self-Driving ，完全⾃动驾驶）即将真正落地中国市场的相关猜测。
图⽚来源：特斯拉官⽹

根据招聘信息，此次开放的岗位包括智驾测试（实⻋测试）技师、智驾测试⼯程师、智驾场地
测试专员等，办公地点覆盖北京、上海、⼴州、深圳、苏州、武汉等 9 个城市。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

据悉，上述岗位均⾪属于 Autopilot （⾃动辅助驾驶）研发部⻔，职责涉及实⻋测试、法规跟踪、
跨团队协作等，岗位内容提及加速完全⾃动驾驶、主动安全功能、 Autopilot 整⻋级测试等。其
中， “ 跟踪中国认证和监管法规变化 ” 也被明确写⼊岗位职责，且相关岗位均标注 “ 急聘 ” 。
据《每⽇经济新闻》记者搜索发现，⽬前智驾测试（实⻋测试）技师与智驾测试⼯程师两个岗
位均可在第三⽅招聘⽹站查到，但不同平台显⽰的薪资范围有所不同。
以智驾测试（实⻋测试）技师为例，在 BOSS 直聘上显⽰薪资为 9000 元 ~1.4 万元，⽽在猎聘上
的薪资范围则为 1 万元 ~3 万元。此外，智驾场地测试专员职位⽬前仅能在特斯拉中国招聘官⽹进
⾏投递。
值得注意的是，上述岗位涉及的 AEB 、 FCW 等功能，均属于当前国内主动安全强制标准的重要
组成部分。随着相关强制性国家标准逐步推进实施，⻋企在中国市场的智能驾驶测试与验证体
系，也需要与本地法规要求进⼀步对⻬。
有观点认为，此次招聘同时覆盖策略、场地执⾏及⽇常路测等多个层⾯，说明特斯拉并⾮简单

补充⼈员，⽽是在系统性推进 FSD 本⼟化测试。尤其是测试岗位覆盖北上⼴深等多个核⼼城
市，也意味着其测试⽹络正在进⼀步铺开，被视为特斯拉加速智驾⽅案本⼟适配的重要信号。
5 ⽉ 21 ⽇早盘，⽆⼈驾驶概念股集体异动，盘中⼀度涨超 1.7% ，⼤众交通、索菱股份、德赛西
威、浙江世宝等多只个股涨停。
与资本市场的热情相⽐， FSD 在中国市场的实际落地进展仍较为有限。记者就相关情况向特斯
拉中国⽅⾯求证，但截⾄发稿未获回复。
与此同时，终端⽤⼾⽬前得到的反馈依然是 “ 继续等待 ” 。⼀位于 2025 年 3 ⽉末购⼊特斯拉 Model
3 的⻋主向记者表⽰，他于 5 ⽉ 21 ⽇上午曾向特斯拉官⽅客服咨询是否可以通过⽀付 6.4 万元开通
智能辅助驾驶功能，官⽅客服回应称： “ ⽬前正积极推进准备⼯作，⼀旦所有准备⼯作完成便会
尽快推送。 ”
⽽在⼀线销售⻔店层⾯，所谓 “FSD ⼊华 ” 的计划⽬前也尚未真正落实。记者在⾛访特斯拉北京
⼀家线下⻔店时了解到，销售⼈员暂未收到 FSD 正式推送的相关通知。⽬前，仅部分⻔店试驾

⻋搭载了 FSD V13 版本，可供⽤⼾体验，但该版本尚不具备⾃动倒⻋等完整能⼒。
事实上，从 FSD 近两年的产品演进来看，当前中国市场与北美市场之间仍存在⼀定差距。公开
信息显⽰，特斯拉 FSD V13 版本最早于 2024 年 12 ⽉底向北美市场推送，新增 “ ⼀键启动 FSD” 功
能。⻋辆⽆需⼈⼯操作，即可⾃主完成从⻋位启动、倒⻋驶出停⻋场，到最终⾃动泊⼊⽬标⻋
位的全过程。当前在售 Model Y 、 Model 3 ⻋型采⽤的屏幕倒挡设计，也被外界视为是为这⼀功
能进⾏的适配。⽬前， FSD V14.3.3 监督版已在北美推送。
相⽐之下， FSD 在中国市场的推进节奏则显得更为谨慎。 2024 年 9 ⽉，特斯拉 AI 团队通过官⽅社
交媒体账号公布了 FSD 全球部署路线图。按照规划，特斯拉原计划于 2025 年第⼀季度在中国和
欧洲市场推出 FSD 功能。
从功能推⼴路径来看，该功能⾃ 2025 年 2 ⽉正式推出以来，其名称已从最初的 “ 完全⾃动驾驶能
⼒ ” ，调整为 “FSD 智能辅助驾驶功能 ” ，随后⼜进⼀步更名为 “ 智能辅助驾驶系统 ” 。

### Page 3

图⽚来源：特斯拉中国官⽹
与此同时，特斯拉在国内的功能开放也经历多次调整：其曾于去年 2 ⽉上线城市道路 Autopilot 功
能，并于 3 ⽉启动 FSD 限时体验活动，但仅⼀周后便暂停。此后，在 2025 年三季度财报电话会
上，特斯拉 CEO ⻢斯克进⼀步透露， FSD 预计将于 2026 年⼀季度获得中国市场审批。
实际上，为推动 FSD 进⼊中国市场， 2021 年，特斯拉便在上海临港设⽴数据中⼼，实现数据本
地化存储。今年 2 ⽉，特斯拉公司全球副总裁陶琳进⼀步对外表⽰，位于上海临港的特斯拉 AI 训
练中⼼已正式投⼊使⽤，完成了 “ 数据存储 — 本地训练 — 算法优化 ” 的全链路本⼟化。
据陶琳透露，⽬前特斯拉中国⻋队累计已积累超过 30 亿公⾥本⼟道路数据， FSD 针对中国市场
本地化调优，并不完全依赖真实道路数据采集。
图⽚来源：每经记者  刘曦  摄
公开信息显⽰，截⾄ 2026 年第⼀季度末，特斯拉 FSD 全球订阅⽤⼾已达到 128 万，同⽐增⻓
51% 。⾃ 2026 年 2 ⽉起，特斯拉还在多数海外市场将 FSD 转向 99 美元 / ⽉（约合⼈⺠币 673 元 / ⽉）

的订阅模式，以进⼀步提升⽤⼾渗透率。⽽在中国市场⽬前为⼀次性购买，价格仍维持在 6.4 万
元。
业内分析认为， FSD 落地将对中国智能驾驶产业带来深远影响。光⼤证券在研报中指出，特斯
拉 FSD ⼊华⾯临本地化不⾜的挑战。与国内智驾第⼀梯队相⽐， FSD 在通⽤场景下表现更拟
⼈，但在本⼟化应变能⼒上仍待加强。
在技术路线层⾯，特斯拉 FSD 采⽤纯视觉⽅案与端到端神经⽹络架构，不依赖⾼精地图，将与
当前中国主流⻋企普遍采⽤的 “ 多传感器融合 + 激光雷达 ” 路线形成正⾯竞争。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款

‘ 是好事 ’ ，能够形成良性竞争并推动⾏业整体进步，⼩鹏也欢迎这种⾼⽔平对⽐。 ”
在他看来，⼩鹏相较特斯拉的优势主要体现在中国本地化场景适配、本⼟数据积累及本地算⼒

势。

熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《高油價影響通膨及降息路徑 ，若降息機會降低將
不利公用事業評價，反之則有提升空間》
美伊達成短期停火協議 ，然荷姆茲海峽實際運量並
未恢復至先前水準 ，使得油價仍在每桶 100美元以
上，也讓通膨及降息路徑更難預測 。未來若油價 、
通膨持續向上 ，降息機會降低將不利於公用事業產
業評價，反之則使公用事業產業評價有提升空間。
公用事業屬於資本密集型產業 ，極度依賴債務融資
來支撐電網更新與基礎設施建設 ，降息預期升高將
顯著降低企業的利息支出 ，直接修復利潤空間 。此
外，當無風險利率 （如美債殖利率 ）下行時，公用
事業股穩定的現金流與高股息率將再次獲得避險資

金青睞，重新奪回「類債券資產 」的定價優勢 。整
體而言，公用事業在不穩定情境下較具防禦性 ，且
先前指數價格已回落 ，目前公用事業產業具成長空
間。

《美伊衝突加速全球能源轉型》
美伊衝突重塑了美國的能源戰略邏輯 。當荷姆茲海
峽的封鎖威脅到全球 20%的石油供應時 ，能源政策
的焦點已從單純的 「低碳」轉向了更為迫切的 「能
源自主」，美伊衝突將加速全球能源轉型。
面對中東化石燃料的高度不確定性 ，核能因其零碳
排且具備高穩定性的特質 ，將使全球加速使用核能
發電。為了支持AI資料中心猛烈增長的電力需求 ，

政府與企業正加速重啟舊核電廠並佈署小型模組化
反應爐，核能被視為國家安全級別的不中斷電源 。
另外，戰爭引發的通膨雖然短期內抬高了再生能源
的建設成本 ，但長遠來看 ，也加速了美國與中東能
源供應的脫鉤 。公用事業公司正利用這段時期 ，將
分散式電網( 包括家用太陽能與儲能 )與發電系統結
合，構建一個受國際油價波動影響較小的內部循環
系統。
2
資料來源：世界核能協會，2026/1，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/4/29

## Document 265: 【天然資源產業攻略】天然資源受惠價格上漲

熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《油價短期仍偏強勢 ，若開放未來回落程度也有限 》
受美伊朗衝突和荷姆茲海峽中斷影響致海灣國家減
產，OPEC4月石油產量月減83萬桶/日至2,004萬桶，
創2000年來的最低 ，僅阿聯酋 (UAE)因利用阿曼灣
出口而增產 ，UAE也擬5/1退出OPEC，以便將產量
從現約350萬桶/日(產能約430萬桶/日)提高到明年
的500萬桶/日。預期整體OPEC+(含NON-OPEC，
不含伊朗、委內瑞拉及利比亞)產能佔全球油市供給
42%，UAE退出將使整體 OPEC+可掌控產能降至
37.6%，OPEC+決議6月配額仍持續增加 18.8萬桶/

日，但現受海峽封鎖影響 ，此兩部分影響均低 ，之
後除非OPEC+解體才會使油價低檔缺乏支撐 ，否則
預估今年 OPEC+對油價的調節掌控性仍強 。2Q26
原油受中東繞道出口 、生產下降、下游減產降需求 、
巴西增產、海上油輪陸續到港、釋儲及延長豁免對
市場投資展望
資產 2Q26看法 投資展望
能源 中立 能源價格短期偏強，即使海峽開放，後續因供應鏈干擾及庫存回補使
能源價格仍相對偏高，美國能源業為相對受惠者。
原物料 正向 美商品化學、化肥、鋁價呈短多。受供給下降，今年銅價維持高檔。
金價短承壓但仍維持中多展望。
• 能源：能源價格短期偏強，即使
海峽開放，後續因供應鏈干擾及
庫存回補使能源價格仍相對偏高。

俄羅斯原油制裁使 3月油市缺口低於預期 ，4月庫存
降幅擴大，預估庫存營運壓力點在 6月，2Q26布油
均價高於100美元，若3Q26油輪通行量未回升，布
油將在 110美元之上 ，若六月大幅放行 ，受能源設
備毀損修復數月 、港口活動回常態約需兩個月及急
於回補庫存，3Q26布油僅回落至90-100美元間。
《預期歐亞天然氣價格優於去年同期，美持平》
海峽封鎖及卡達液化天然氣 (LNG)關閉，降低LNG
供給近兩成 ，若海峽大幅開放 ，LNG至客戶端約需
3~6個月，部分設備毀損需 3~5年修復 ，使LNG市
場今、明年轉供需吃緊，遞延擴產浪潮至2027年底
~2028年。因美天然氣終端維護計畫及氣候影響需
求使近期出口下降，但預期氣溫轉熱及2Q26產能上

線增出口量 ，預估美天然氣價格持平去年 。歐、亞
洲天然氣儲量偏低，須靠降需求、轉/增其他燃料發
電、多元化供應源以回補庫存供冬季使用 ，歐、亞
天然氣價格將淡季不淡，優於去年同期。
重點摘要
• 天然氣：受中東 LNG出口下
降及歐 、亞洲天然氣庫存偏
低，預期歐 、亞洲天然氣價
格將 優於去年同期 ，美天然
氣受2Q26起陸續上線增加出
口能力及歐 、亞洲庫存回補
需求 ，預期美國天然氣價格
持平去年同期。
• 煉油廠擁超額利潤 ，油田服
務業者受惠能源安全的開發
需求 ，整合性能源商受惠能
源價格高漲 ，加上資本紀律 ，
有利美國能源業現金流成長
及股東回報。
下圖/天然氣價格美相對持平，但歐洲春夏仍偏高
資料來源：高盛，2026/4/20，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/5/11
2
天然氣價格走勢預估
《能源價格高漲及商機增加有利美能源商》
受煉油廠關廠 ，原油供應中斷使煉油減產 ，成品油
庫存去化短缺風險高於原油 ，產量供應下降以柴油
及航空燃料降幅最高 ，裂解價差漲幅也高於汽油 ，

多國限制成品油出口 。預期若煉油廠開工率提升 ，
成品油供應仍緊張 ，超額利潤雖縮水 ，但仍遠優於
去年同期 ，有利煉油廠 。受全球重視能源安全 ，上
游看好國際深水與離岸市場前景 ，加上美有機會參
與委國投資及維修商機 ，也有利美國油服業 。美能
源商雖部分中東投資因戰事受損減少獲利 ，但受惠
能源商品價格偏高 、煉油裂解價差擴增及 LNG出口
量增加，市場大幅上修美國能源商獲利 ，維持資本
紀律，使現金流仍強大 ，維持股息成長及股票回購 ，
但因能源股股價走勢與能源產品價格相關 ，若能源
價格回跌為下行風險，對美國能源股持中立。

《美商品化學 、化肥呈短多 ，海峽開放為下行風險 》
商品化學1Q26雖聚乙烯銷量成長，但被部分地區下
游停產 、維護及工業需求下降使財報低於預期 ，
2Q26展望因中東供應持續中斷 、4月上調聚乙烯 、
丙烯等報價，將使2Q26商品化學獲利增加；受天然
氣價漲使歐洲肥料廠商減產 ，中東供給中斷影響印
度、巴基斯坦 、孟加拉等尿素生產受限 ，中國實施
尿素出口配額制，利多2Q26美氮肥廠商獲利，但若
海峽開放為商品化學 、氮肥價格的下行風險 ，然實
質上亞洲商品化學廠受物流影響海峽開放後到復產
需一季以上，預期3Q26商品化學供應仍緊，今年氮
肥市場供給趨緊 ，恐影響4Q26亞/非洲糧食種植 ，
為明年穀物價格的上行風險。塗料廠1Q26財報顯示

銷量持平 (航空、航太成長 ，但車市疲軟 )，主要受
惠價格上漲 、產品組合及匯率 ，使財報優於預期 ，
預估2Q26受價格上漲、銷量呈現低個位數成長，營
收呈中低個位數成長 ，2Q26獲利優於1Q26，今年
受價格上揚抵銷銷量疲軟下調及原料成本上揚 ，維
持中低個位數銷售成長 ，預期2H26獲利優於1H26。
資料來源：高盛、各公司，2026/5/1 ，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/5/5
下圖/若開放海峽通行，多數需一季以上才達客戶端
重點摘要
• 化學：美伊戰降低中東 、亞
洲供給及受原料上揚推升產
品價格 ，美商品化學 、化肥
呈短多 ，但海峽大幅開放為
股價下行風險。
• 鋼鐵：美鋼廠受高關稅保護
及鋼價上漲而獲利成長 。但
受供給增加 ，預估鐵礦砂價
2Q26持平，2H26走弱。
《美鋼廠獲利成長；受供給漸增使鐵礦砂價持平轉弱》
受美國高關稅 ，成品鋼進口市佔率已降至 15%，推
升美國本土鋼鐵價格上漲 ，且因進口替代及需求改
善，使1Q26美鋼廠財報優於預期，預估能源、資料

中心和基礎設施領域支撐鋼鐵需求使積壓訂單量增 ，
上調年度鋼鐵出貨預估，預期2Q26美境內鋼廠將受
惠出貨量改善及鋼價漲幅超過廢鋼上漲 ，提升利潤
率呈獲利成長 。中國重點城市推出放寬買房限制 、
買房補貼和提高貸款成數等政策 ，使中國百大房企
今年前四月銷售雖呈現下降 ，降幅縮減，鋼鐵供需
較上月放緩，4月鋼鐵PMI低於50%，預期製造業及
基建領域為今年鋼鐵需求成長動能 。近期鐵礦砂價
到岸價(因運費上揚)上漲，FOB價持穩，港口鐵礦

《供給端擾動使2Q26銅、鋁價強勢》
受印尼銅礦復產遞延 、若中東硫磺供給無法恢復 ，
恐影響3Q26銅生產，加上祕魯頒發能源危機緊急法
令限制非民生用電 ，恐限縮礦山供給 ，推動銅價 近
新高 ，但中國需求增速放緩 ，銅庫存下降 ，預期
2Q26銅價維持高檔，有利於抵銷銅礦商產量下調的
壓力。受中東鋁供給中斷及部分產能毀損需數月才
能復產，預期2Q26鋁價將偏強勢，印尼新供給將在
2H26增加或海峽通航才能舒緩壓力。
資料來源：JP Morgan，2026/5/10，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/5/13

重點摘要
• 銅價受供給端下降壓力及中
國需求回溫 ，庫存下降推動
2Q26銅價維持高檔；鋁價因
中東供給下降而強勢。
• 黃金：短期金價仍受利率預
期變動而承壓 ，但實質利率
下降 、去交易對手風險 、政
治干預資產 、多元化資產配
置仍使 中期金價處上行趨勢 。
4
《金價短期承壓，但結構性需求支撐仍呈中多》
近月受市場權衡美伊停戰可能性對油價推升通膨程
度而影響降息預期 ，政策顯示高利率恐持續較久及
部分央行賣出黃金支撐流動性 ，金價先反彈後承壓 。
預估短期仍受利率預期變化而承壓 。雖預期降息延
後，但若處停滯型通膨環境 ，實質利率下降 ，對金
價有利；川普頻以關稅 、軍事力量施壓各國 、政策
反覆增添經貿不確定性 ，加上軍事行動推升財政壓
力及擔憂債務可持續性 。市場尋求去交易對手風險 、
政治干預資產及多元化資產配置 ，雖部分央行可能
處分黃金儲備 ，但如中國及波蘭等央行續增加黃金
儲備，1Q26全球央行購金量仍高於均值，預期今年
黃金購買量，接近去年；1Q26黃金ETF投資雖淨賣

超，但亞洲呈淨買入 ，預估今年仍增加 。預期中期
金價仍處上行趨勢。
資料來源：世界黃金協會，2026/4/29，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/5/12

## Document 279: 【金融產業攻略】短線消化地緣通膨，中期成長韌性仍在

Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
短線消化地緣通膨，中期成長
韌性仍在
熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
• 管理層看好經濟韌性，惟地緣風
險潛伏需審慎監測 pg1
• 監管新規將使多數銀行受惠 pg2
• 美大型銀行看淡私募信貸風險 pg2
重點摘要
本月關鍵圖表
• 美國銀行業管理層一致認為美
國經濟具備韌性，消費者收支
與企業營運維持穩健，工商業
貸款持續成長，強勁的股市表
現預示著財富管理和投資銀行
業務的積極前景。新提出的資
本新規對所有的銀行皆屬有利，
又大型銀行皆表態並不擔憂私
募信貸市場，有助金融股中期
成長趨勢延續。
• 由於財報利多已出盡，加上通
膨數據與聯準會降息預期遞延，
使市場將焦點轉向錯綜的地緣
政治與通膨風險的不確定性，
壓抑金融股短線表現，2Q26中

立看待。
市場投資展望
資產 2Q26看法 投資展望
金融 中立 美金融股中期成長韌性依舊，短線估值靜待地緣與通膨風暴消化
1
《美金融股 Q1業績強勁 ，信貸質量平穩 、管理層
看好經濟韌性，惟地緣風險潛伏需審慎監測》
美大型金融股 1Q26業績強勁並優於市場預期 ，增
長動能遍及各業務 ，包括市場交易 (市場波動激勵
股票交易成長 )、投資銀行業務 (受惠併購與股票承
銷活動成長 )、資產管理業務 (受惠管理資產規模擴
大)、淨利息收入 (受惠存款、貸款雙成長 、固定利
率證券到期後以較高利率進行再投資所帶來的持續
性收益 )、支付業務等 ，且信貸質量平穩可控 。各
家銀行管理層也一致表示美國經濟依然保持韌性 ，
消費者收入和支出持續成長，企業營運依然穩健。
為金融股奠定良好的成長趨勢基石。
然自第一季財報發布後，銀行股表現落後大盤，除
了歸因於財報利多出盡，另一個的原因是市場焦點
轉往日益複雜的地緣發展、能源價格上漲對通膨和
經濟成長的影響上。因4月美國消費者物價指數開

重點摘要
2
始反映油價居高不下的壓力 ，Fed降息預期因通膨
仍高而遞延 。然，近期市場的擔憂仍言之過早 ，目
前的高利率環境對銀行淨息差仍具助益 。且摩根大
通資料顯示 2Q26(截至5/6)貸款仍在加速 ，中高所
得族群和退稅優惠使消費者支出成長依然穩健 ，而
高盛的資料則顯示併購活動也在持續成長中 ，截至
5/11，第二季大型銀行公告的購併案件數較去年成
長13%，費用收入成長62%。
《監管新規將使多數大型銀行受惠》
此次財報各家管理層亦針對監管新規的影響 (巴塞爾
協議III最終方案和全球系統重要性銀行(G-SIB)提案)
提出看法 ，摩根大通表示擬議規則下對資本要求將
增加約 4%，而聯準會對大型銀行的估計將減少約

5%，這意味著摩根大通往美國家庭和企業提供的信
貸成本可能高於其他非全球系統重要性銀行 (G-SIB)
的國內信貸成本 ，不利國內外競爭力 。美國銀行表
示提案框架若通過 ，未來一段時間的整體資本要求
可能會比現行制度降低 。高盛對監理改革的方向感
到鼓舞。花旗表示對集團將產生淨效益。
《美大型銀行看淡私募信貸風險 ，募資與動能仍強 》
另一個備受關注的議題則是私募信貸市場 ，摩根大
通表示對其並不特別擔憂 ，也表示公司尚未看到重
大損失。美國銀行表示目前的市場活動很大程度上
是流動性的重新定價 ，以及對另類資產管理公司增
長預期的調整 ，而非系統性信用減損的跡象 。高盛
表示龐大的機構客戶群和強大的客戶參與度支撐了
私募信貸和另類投資募資業務的持續成長。
資料來源：高盛，2026/4/24，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/5/19

摘要：
伊朗局势主导资产⾛势。美股早盘中东局势紧张，⼀度打压美股⾛低。午盘报道称协议最终草
案达成，原油跳⽔、三⼤指数探底回升、⼩幅收涨，道指创新⾼。美债则在油价回落和强劲宏
观数据下⾛势分化。
（美股债油⽇内⾛势）
霍尔⽊兹海峡局势依然是市场最核⼼的⻛险变量。交易员全天紧盯谈判进展，油价与股市随每
⼀则报道剧烈摇摆：
值得注意的是， WTI 前⽉合约已连续第⼆个交易⽇收于 50 ⽇移动均线下⽅，上次出现这⼀技术信
号是在 4 ⽉ 17 ⽇的阶段低点。
美股盘前，据央视新闻，有消息⼈⼠表⽰，伊朗最⾼领袖已经下令，接近武器级的铀必须留

在伊朗。伊朗拒绝有关放弃本国浓缩铀储备的表述，推动油价⼀度跳涨 4% 。
美股早盘，美伊核谈判僵持，纳指跌 0.5% ，道指跌 0.3% 。投资者再度陷⼊等待，观望中东和
平谈判能否从预期落地为实质进展。
美股午盘，华尔街⻅闻提及，据伊朗媒体援引中东的消息，在巴基斯坦斡旋下，美国与伊朗
协议的最终草案已经达成，预计将在未来数⼩时内公布。
受消息刺激，美国 WTI 原油⼀度跌⾄ 95.76 美元、⽇内跌超 2.5% ，布油⽇内跌逾 2.7% 。美股
三⼤指数则全线反攻，由跌转涨。
美股尾盘，伊朗总统表⽰伊朗 " 绝不退让 " 、 " 不会被导弹、炸弹或战机征服 " ，油价再度回升，
WTI 前⽉合约最终基本持平。美股三⼤指数回落，纳指惊险收涨，道指则仍收创新⾼。

英伟达跌 1.8% ，领跌科技七巨头，隔夜收盘后公布的业绩被市场普遍视为强劲，但股价仍延续
其过去财报后承压的规律。
我们⽬前处于较⾼的估值⽔平，部分由强劲盈利驱动。随着财报季基本结束，市场不会再出现意
外的盈利惊喜，注意⼒已重新转向伊朗局势。短期⽽⾔，市场⾛向将由涉伊朗的传⾔或实质性协
议来决定。

### Page 3

（英伟达股价⽇内⾛势）

在任何单⼀交易⽇都难以依靠业绩数据撬动股价，这⼀规律在过去六⾄七个季度中反复验证。
沃尔玛股价⼤跌 7.3% ，成为当⽇表现最差的主要权重股之⼀，并拖累消费必需品板块整体下跌
1.6% 。

David Rainey 表⽰：
受沃尔玛拖累，Casey's General Stores跌 3.3% ，Costco Wholesale下跌 2.2% 。
彭博策略师援引数据指出，随着股市对油价的敏感度重回⼀个⽉前⽔平，沃尔玛的警⽰进⼀步
印证了能源价格⾼企向消费端的传导压⼒。
量⼦计算相关股票成为当⽇最⼤赢家，在特朗普政府拟向多家量⼦计算公司授予芯⽚法案补贴
的消息刺激下，相关个股普遍⼤涨。
消费者正承受⾼油价带来的压⼒，若⾼成本环境持续，预计第⼆季度及下半年零售价格通胀将有

所⾛⾼。

定，CDS 走勢平穩，是目前債券投資
人眼中相對穩健且具收斂空間的標的 。

▋ 美債利率 Q2 持續易升難降 
2026 年 4 月 FOMC 維持基準利率於 3.5%
至 3.75%不變 ， 符合市場預期 ， 連續第三次
會議按兵不動，也是現任 Fed 主席鮑威爾
最後一次以主席身分 ， 出席FOMC。此次會
議官員間意見更為分歧，理事 Miran 照慣
例維持鴿派立場 ， 投下支持降息1 碼的票 ，
另有 3 位票委反對在聲明中保留「寬鬆傾
向」 的措辭，認為應在聲明中，表達雙向立
場 ， 意即當前雖仍維持降息循環 ， 但不排除
在特定情況下轉為升息 ， 因此此次會議投票
結果為 8：4，為 1992 年以來分歧最嚴重
的一場會議。

受伊朗戰爭影響，油價自 2 月底以來上漲
超過 60%，雖然 3 月 CPI 數據顯示當前能
源價格尚未擴散至其他消費品 ， 不過 ， 荷姆
茲海峽從美伊熱戰轉為雙方僵持不下 ，國際
海事組織(IMO)報告顯示 ， 仍約有1,600 艘
各國商船滯留在波斯灣內 ， 行政院於5/4 報
告中也表示其中包含 8 艘台灣籍船舶 ， 截至
5 月上旬，荷莫茲海峽日通行量仍低於 10
艘，相較美伊開戰前的約 100 艘，目前運
量明顯不足(圖二)，物價壓力仍有持續堆疊
的可能。因此，截至5/6，Fed Watch 顯示
Fed 今年升息 1 碼機率已升至約兩成(圖三)。

中 長 期 美 債 殖 利 率 走 勢 將 視 新 任 主 席
Warsh 於 5/16 接任後， 對於物價目標、基
準利率 、 資產負債表改革的態度 。 物價目標
部分，Warsh 更看重剃除極端目標的截尾
均值物價指數(Trimmed-mean PCE)， 將更
淡化油價短線大幅波動對物價變動的影響
或創造長期有更多降息空間 。不過 ， 制度面
不易於甫上任就大幅更改 ，評估短期美債殖
利率在反應政策利率難以調降下，第 2 季
將持續維持高檔整理。

圖二：荷姆茲海峽每日通行船舶數急凍

資料來源：Bloomberg, 5/6/2026

圖三：聯準會今年升息機率已高於降息

資料來源：Bloomberg, 5/6/2026

▋ 雲端巨頭大發債，但仍有好標的 
截至 5/7， 雖然美國公債殖利率持續於高位
整理 ， 惟因市場風險偏好回升 ， 加上美國投
等債殖利率突破 5%甜蜜點，資金回補投等
債 ， 美國投資等級債利差於近一個月收斂約
5bs，各產業中，第1 季因私募信貸利空表
現較差的投資級銀行債、 保險債，因Q1 財
報公布緩解市場對私募信貸暴雷擔憂 ， 相關
券種於近一個月顯著出現反彈(圖一)。 
不過 ， 隨著美國公債殖利率持續易升難降 ，
美國投資等級債整體利差要再收窄空間評

估相對有限 ， 因此選債方面 ， 可挑選仍具有
收斂空間的券種介入。

美國 5 大雲端巨頭在 Q1 財報公布後 ， 市場
紛紛上修資本支出預估 ， 今年預估值上修至
8,050 億美元，明年更進一步上修至 1.116
兆，突破兆元大關(圖四)。因此，除以本身
的獲利因應以外，5 大 CSP 企業從去年第
４季以來就開始大幅發債(圖五)，提前儲備
今明兩年所需要的資金，投資級科技債供給
增加造成利差收斂幅度相對其他券種有限 。

圖四：美國 5 大 CSP 廠資本支出大增

資料來源：Morgan Stanly, 5/6/2026

圖五：AI 企業發債量持續居高不下

資料來源：Bloomberg, 5/6/2026

不過 ， 目前市場對於科技巨頭發債 、 擴大資
本支出的反應不盡相同 ， 以Alphabet*為例 ，
於今年 2 月發行的公司債 ，獲得超10 倍認
購額，但 Meta*於 4/30 所發行的公司債，
則僅獲得約 3.5 倍的認購額 。以 Q1 財報公
布結果也可觀察到 ，Alphabet*雖上調今年
資本支出至 1,750-1,850 億美元 ， 年增率高
達 97%， 但也展現投資AI 產生可見的獲利
及現金流 ， 因此財報公布隔日後股價上漲以
對，反之，Meta*、Microsoft*等，雖然資
本支出也上調 ， 但因變現能力仍有疑慮 ， 財
報公布隔日後股價下跌(圖六)。以主要 CPS
廠的債券CDS 走勢觀察 ，Alphabet*的CDS
最為平穩 ， 近半年增幅有限 ， 顯示其為目前
債券投資人眼中，相對穩定的標的。

圖六：市場不怕支出多，只要變現能力強

資料來源：公司數據, 5/6/2026

圖七：Alphabet* CDS 相對平穩

資料來源：Bloomberg, 5/7/2026

▋ 結論 
美國科技債利差具收斂空間 ， 不過各發債人
間狀況各異 ， 可挑選資本支出雖高但市場認
可其獲利能力的相關科技債。
